===PAGE 44=== (ocr, 32 lines)
   1|             Articulation Agreements with local colleges also play an important role in helping reduce the cost of higher education.
   2|              Qualified Monty Tech students are eligible to receive college credits through a number of articulation agreements with
   3|             public and private colleges across the country. Just over 70% of the graduating class of 2012 reported plans to enroll
   4|             at either a 2-year college, 4-year college/university, or a technical/trade school upon graduation. By earning college
   5|             credits while still in high school, these students will benefit by saving both time and money as they pursue advanced
   6|             educational programs.
   7|                                                            Financial Report
   8|             Numerous challenges were faced during the development of the fiscal year 2011-2012 budget, including rising health
   9|             insurance and student transportation costs. Considerable effort was put forth by the School Committee,
  10|             administration, and staff to develop a cost-effective budget. The final fiscal year 2011-2012 Educational Plan totaled
  11|             $22,744,779, which represents a $2.4% increase over the 2010-2011 Educational Plan.
  12|             The District was audited in August 2012 as part of the yearly financial audit by the accounting firm of Melanson, Heath
  13|             and Co. from Greenfield, MA and an "excellent" report is anticipated.
  14|                                                          Grants and Contracts
  15|             Monty Tech continues to pursue grant funding on an annual basis. These funds assist in providing many educational
  16|             and social services to the student population. For fiscal year 2012, state and federal grant sources provided the school
  17|             with $1,407,959. Programs funded by these grants include: Essential Health Services, Social Intervention and
  18|             Mediation, Improving Teacher Quality, Special Education Services and Program Improvement, Title | Support, Drug Free
  19|             Schools, Perkins Occupational Education, and Summer Academic Support.
  20|             In addition to state and federal grant sources, Monty Tech was also the recipient of private and competitive grant
  21|             awards totaling $20,918. For Fiscal Year 2012, Monty Tech received $13,251 in grant funds from the Community
  22|             Foundation of North Central Massachusetts, to support the Student Spaceflight Experiment Program.         The
  23|             Massachusetts Space Grant Consortium and Nypro also contributed to this ground-breaking program, by adding an
  24|             additional $7,667 in grant funds.
  25|             Grants totaled $1,428,877 for fiscal year 2012.

  17|                                                            Special Services
  18|             During the 2011-2012 school year, Montachusett Regional Vocational Technical School District provided special
  19|             services to over three hundred students - measuring progress of approximately two hundred students on Individual
  20|             Education Plans (IEPs) and just over one hundred students adhering to individualized 504 plans. While the Student
  21|             Support Services (SSS) Department encompasses special education, the department provides support and is available
  22|             to all Monty Tech students.
  23|             The department incudes a full-time nursing staff that administers medications, performs state-mandated health
  24|             screening exams, and provides, when necessary, health information to the special education team for a student's IEP
  25|             meeting. The department is fortunate to be able to support a full-time school social worker who participates in
  26|             departmental meetings and assists students who have needs concerning finances, family issues, homelessness,
  27|             maternity, health issues, and proper food and clothing. The school is fortunate to have on staff a full-time psychologist,
  28|            whose role it is to evaluate all students referred for an initial evaluation or who require a three-year re-evaluation. In
  29|             addition, we have a full-time speech pathologist, who is available to assist students with disabilities, assess these
  30|            students and consult with teachers. Our students also have access to the services of a full-time adjustment counselor
  31|            and part-time school psychologist. All of these individuals are available for scheduled counseling sessions and mental
  32|            health emergency treatment, as well as crisis intervention.
  33|            The school's Director of Student Support Services oversees the district's Special Education Program, which is
  34|            reviewed annually in May, in accordance with regulatory requirements. This comprehensive review and evaluation is
  35|            done in collaboration with the Parent Advisory Council, and the results of the evaluation are used to improve the
  36|            special education procedures and programs in place at Montachusett Regional Vocational Technical School.

  26|                  Qualified applicants spent two days a month working on real-world work projects under the mentorship of company
  27|                 managers at SimplexGrinnell and Tyco Safety Products, subsidiaries of Tyco International, a Fortune 500 company.

  29|                • Gardner and Narragansett - participate in the program, working on a wide variety of projects. Their projects are

   1|                  In March 2012 the postgraduate program successfully graduated our first class of fifteen Emergency Medical
   2|                  Technicians. The students took their practical exam at Monty Tech and proceeded to take their written exam at a state- designated facility. Several graduates have already found employment in their chosen field.
   3|                  The Director of the Postgraduate & Continuing Studies Program actively seeks information to develop new (and expand
   4|                  existing) certificate and licensure programs that align with regional workforce needs and employment trends.
   5|                                                            Practical Nursing Program
   6|                  The Practical Nursing Program is a post-secondary licensure program, offered through the Postgraduate & Continuing
   7|                  Studies Department. The widely popular program is designed to prepare graduates to practice safely, ethically and in a
   8|                 caring manner for patients who are experiencing common variations in health status in diverse health care settings.
   9|                 In October 2010, Monty Tech's Practical Nursing Program was reviewed by the Massachusetts Board of Nursing and
  10|                 received full approval status. The program has since maintained that accreditation, and in June 2011 the program
  11|                 is scheduled for a full review and site visit by COE in November 2012. became a candidate for accreditation by the Council on Occupational Education (COE). The Practical Nursing Program
  12|                 In June 2012, the Practical Nursing Program graduated twenty-eight students bound for the nursing profession. Upon
  13|                 completion of the program, the cohort achieved an initial NCLEX pass rate of 94%, up from 87.5% in 2011. The program
  14|                 is pleased to announce that over one third of our graduates have elected to continue their education, in pursuit of a
  15|                bachelor's degree in nursing through the LPN to BSN Bridge program at Fitchburg State University.
  16|                                                       The Monty Tech School Committee

  18|                individuals, whose expertise proves invaluable in advising the district's operations, policies, and procedures.

  14|                ARTICLE 17. To see if the Town will vote to amend the Zoning Bylaw by adding to the current bylaw Section 4.0, entitled
  15|                "Use Regulations," a new Section entitled Section 4.15, "Solar Energy Systems," or take any other action relative
  16|                thereto, as follows. [Submitted by the Planning Board] Board of Selectmen recommend approval. Finance Committee
  17|                reports no direct financial impact.
  18|                Section 4.15 SOLAR ENERGY SYSTEMS
  19|                4.15.1 Purpose
  20|                The purpose of this section is to provide siting for solar photovoltaic energy systems for power generation for all,
  21|                home, commercial or industrial installations. Small-Scale Ground-Mounted Photovoltaic Installations, Roof-Mounted
  22|                Solar Energy Installations and Large- Scale Ground-Mounted Photovoltaic Installations within the Solar Overlay District
  23|                may be constructed without need for discretionary approval as set forth herein. Large-Scale Ground-Mounted
  24|                Photovoltaic Installations outside of the Solar Overlay District may be constructed by special permit provided certain
  25|                criteria as set forth herein are met. The provisions in this section of the Zoning Bylaw shall apply to the construction,
  26|                operation, repair, and/or removal of all solar electric systems, and to physical modifications that materially alter the
  27|                type, configuration, or size of these installations or related equipment.
  28|                4.15.2 Definitions
  29|                Small-Scale Ground-Mounted Photovoltaic Installation: A solar photovoltaic system that is structurally mounted on the
  30|                ground and has a nameplate capacity under 10 KW. Large-Scale Ground-Mounted Photovoltaic Installation: A solar
  31|                photovoltaic system that is structurally mounted on the ground and has a nameplate capacity of 10 kW or greater.
  32|                Rated Nameplate Capacity: The maximum rated output of electric power production of the photovoltaic system in Direct
  33|                Current (DC). Solar Photovoltaic Array: An arrangement of solar photovoltaic panels. Roof-Mounted Solar Energy
  34|                Installation: Solar photovoltaic arrays placed on the roof of residences, or commercial, industrial or institutional
  35|                buildings and Town owned municipal buildings. Buffer Strip: A strip of land between the solar photovoltaic arrays, and
  36|                any structures accessory thereto, and the boundary of the parcel, reserved for plant material, berms, walls or fencing to
  37|                serve as a visual barrier. Plant Material: Trees or shrubs of a type and height that sufficiently screen the arrays without
  38|                blocking necessary sun that would hinder the product performance, including ground cover that would screen the
  39|                lower portion of the arrays.
  40|                4.15.3 Small-Scale Ground-Mounted Solar Photovoltaic Installations and Roof-Mounted Solar Energy Installations
  41|                4.15.3.1 Purpose
  42|                The purpose of section is to promote the creation of renewable energy for individual residences, commercial
  43|                enterprises and municipal buildings, as-of-right.
  44|                4.15.3.2 Roof-Mounted Solar Energy Installations
  45|                Roof-Mounted Solar Energy Installations shall be located so as not to increase the total height of the structure more
  46|                than one (1) foot above the applicable zoning regulations related to height in the District in which it is located, or such
  47|                                                                     81

===PAGE 85=== (ocr, 50 lines)
   1|                   foundation of the primary dwelling is more than 125 feet from the front property line or otherwise if determined
   2|                   appropriate by the Building/Zoning Official. Small-Scale Ground-Mounted Photovoltaic Installation shall be adequately
   3|                   screened from the neighboring lot line as determined by the Building/Zoning Official. A Small-Scale Ground-Mounted
   4|                   Photovoltaic Installation with twenty feet or greater in height, at its maximum extension, shall require a Special Permit
   5|                   in accordance with Section 4.15.4.3.
   6|                    4.15.3.4 Required Documents
   7|                    The following documents shall be required:
   8|                    A. Sun and shadow diagrams specific to the proposed installation to determine the solar access.
   9|                    B. Detailed information, including maps, plans or dimensional sketches showing proposed location of the solar
  10|                   installation, including any setbacks from property lines or distances from structures which are used for habitation on
  11|                   adjacent properties, and a landscape diagram showing proposed screening.
  12|                   C. Site drawings showing the building and structure footprints, property lines, location and the dimensions of solar
  13|                   arrays, ridgeline of roof and description of the installation.
  14|                   D. Elevation drawings showing heights of buildings and solar arrays.
  15|                   4.15.3.5 Permitting
  16|                   Small-Scale Ground-Mounted Solar Photovoltaic Installations and Roof-Mounted Solar Energy Installations require only
  17|                   a building permit, except that a Small-Scale Ground-Mounted Solar Photovoltaic Installation twenty feet in height or
  18|                   greater, at its maximum extension, shall require a special permit in accordance with Section 4.15.4.3. All data listed
  19|                   above shall be subritted to the Building/Zoning Official. All other necessary permits shall be obtained before a building
  20|                   permit is issued.
  21|                   4.15.3.6 As Built Plans
  22|                   As-built plans shall be submitted prior to final inspection to the Building/Zoning Official and copied to the Planning
  23|                   Board.
  24|                   4.15.4 Large-Scale Ground-Mounted Solar Photovoltaic Installations
  25|                   4.15.4.1 Purpose
  26|                   The purpose of this section is to facilitate the creation of new Large-Scale Ground-Mounted Solar Photovoltaic
  27|                   Installations by providing standards for the placement, design, construction, operation, monitoring, modification and
  28|                   removal of such installations that address public safety, minimize impacts on environmental, scenic, natural and
  29|                   historic resources and to provide adequate financial assurance for the eventual decommissioning of such installations.
  30|                   Subject to the requirements below, Large-Scale Ground-Mounted Solar Photovoltaic Installations are permitted as-of-
  31|                   right in the Solar Overlay District and by special permit in any other district. The special permit granting authority for
  32|                   these purposes shall be the Planning Board.
  33|                   4.15.4.2 Solar Overlay District
  34|                   Large-Scale Ground-Mounted Solar Photovoltaic Installations are allowed by right in the following designated Overlay
  35|                   District:
  36|                   1. 27 Youngs Road, 42 Acres, Map 115, Parcel 016 (Commercial District)
  37|                   2. 671 Lancaster Avenue, 132 Acres, Map 110, Parcel 3 (Residence B)
  38|                   3. 500 Leominster Shirley Road, 17.4 Acres, Map 144, Parcel 001 (Office Park and Industrial).
  39|                   4.15.4.3 Siting By Special Permit
  40|                   Except where permitted as-of-right in the Solar Overlay District, Large-Scale Ground-Mounted Solar Photovoltaic
  41|                   Installations may be located on any lot containing more than 20 acres in any district by special permit issued by the
  42|                   Planning Board, provided however that, the requirements of Section 4.15 and the following criteria are met.
  43|                   1. Standards: In granting the special permit, the Planning Board shall find that the permit may be granted without
  44|                   substantial detriment to the public good and without nullifying or substantially derogating from the intent and purpose
  45|                   of this bylaw, and shall find that:
  46|                  - the specific site is an appropriate location for such an installation;
  47|                  - the proposed installation will not adversely affect the existing neighborhood;
  48|                  - the granting of the special permit will not reasonably diminish the available light, air, sunlight and other amenities,
  49|                   and;
  50|                                                                      82

===PAGE 86=== (ocr, 50 lines)
   1|                - screening of premises from view by use of appropriate walls, fencing or butter strips;
   2|                - control of the number, location, size and lighting of signs;
   3|                - additional design and siting modifications where appropriate.
   4|                 4.15.4.4 Development Plan Review
   5|                 All Large-Scale Ground-Mounted Solar Photovoltaic Installations shall require Development Plan Review, under Section
   6|                 8.4 of the Zoning Bylaws, by the Lunenburg Planning Board. A public information meeting shall be held by said Board.
   7|                The Building/Zoning Official shall not issue a building permit unless and until the Planning Board submits an approved
   8|                 Development Plan Review Document, and Special Permit documentation where applicable, to the Building/Zoning
   9|                 Official. A building permit will be issued by the Building/Zoning Official that shows evidence that the project is
  10|                 consistent with state and federal building codes, the findings and directives of the Development Plan Review, andlor
  11|                 Special Permit, and local bylaws and regulations, including those set forth by the Conservation Commission. As-built
  12|                 plans shall be submitted prior to final inspection to the Building Zoning Official with copies to the Planning Board.
  13|                 4.15.4.5 Utility Notification
  14|                 Evidence shall be provided at time of the application for the Development Plan Review that the utility company that
  15|                 operates the electrical grid where the installation is to be located has been informed of the applicant's intent to
  16|                construct a solar photovoltaic installation and that approval to connect to the grid has been granted or appropriate
  17|                application(s) have or will be been made to such utilities for interconnection. Off-grid systems shall be exempt from this
  18|                requirement. Reasonable efforts should be made to place all utility connections underground, depending on
  19|                appropriate soil conditions, shape and topography of the site.
  20|                 4.15.4.6 Fees
  21|                An application for a Development Plan Review shall be accompanied by the required fee and a tri-party account (in-
  22|                house escrow account with the Planning Board, Developer and Town Treasurer) for engineering review, monitoring,
  23|                and inspections fees. An application for a building permit shall be accompanied by the fee required for a building
  24|                permit. All other fees that shall be required by permitting parties (Conservation Commission, etc.) shall be administered
  25|                according to their regulations.
  26|                4.15.4.7 Setbacks and Buffer Strips
  27|                 Buffer Strips in all Districts: All installations shall be surrounded by a buffer strip which shall be two hundred (200) feet
  28|                in depth in a Residential, Conservation or Recreation District and shall be 50 feet in a Commercial or Industrial district
  29|                unless it abuts a Residential, Conservation or Recreation District in which case the buffer strip shall be two hundred
  30|                feet in depth along such abutting lot lines, unless the applicant can demonstrate, and the Planning Board finds, that
  31|                adequate buffering can be provided in a narrower buffer strip.
  32|                Setbacks in all Districts:
  33|                Front Yard: The Front Yard Depth shall be at least 50 feet, provided, however, that when the lot is in a Residential,
  34|                Outlying or Recreation District or abuts a Residential, Conservation, or a Recreation District, the front lot setback shall
  35|                not be less than 200 feet.
  36|                Side Yard: Each side yard setback shall be at least 50 feet, provided, however, that when the lot is in a Residential,
  37|                Outlying or Recreation District or abuts a Residential, Conservation, or a Recreation District, the side yard setback shall
  38|                not be less than 200 feet.
  39|                Rear Yard: The Rear Yard Depth shall be at least 50 feet; provided, when the lot is in a Residential, Outlying or
  40|                Recreation District or abuts a Residential, Conservation, or a Recreation District, the rear yard setback shall not be less
  41|                than 200 feet.
  42|                4.15,4.8 Required Documents
  43|                In addition to documents required for Development Plan Review, the following will be required for Large-Scale Ground-
  44|                Mounted Solar Photovoltaic Installation. The Planning Board may waive one of more of these requirements in its sole
  45|                discretion under appropriate circumstances for Large-Scale Ground-Mounted Solar Photovoltaic Installation between
  46|                10 kW and 249 kW.
  47|                A. All plans and maps shall be prepared, stamped and signed by a professional engineer licensed to practice in
  48|                Massachusetts.

  22|                  4.15.4.9 Design Standards
  23|                  A. Lighting and Security
  24|                  Lighting of solar photovoltaic installations shall be consistent with local, state and federal law. Lighting of other parts
  25|                 of the installation, such as any appurtenant structures, shall be limited to that required for safety and operational
  26|                 purposes and shall be reasonably shielded from abutting properties. Where feasible, any required lighting shall be
  27|                  directed downward and shall incorporate full cut off fixtures to reduce light pollution. Surveillance and security
  28|                  cameras shall be placed as to not infringe on private citizens' property.
  29|                  Signage size shall comply with the Zoning Bylaw and shall not be used for displaying any advertising except to identify
  30|                 the owner and/or operator of the solar installation and a 24-hour emergency contact telephone number.
  31|                  C. Land Clearing
  32|                  Clearing of natural vegetation shall be limited to what is necessary for construction, operation and maintenance of the
  33|                 installation Any land disturbance, including earth removal of land cleared greater than one acre shall be subject to
  34|                  Stormwater Management criteria and/or otherwise prescribed by applicable laws, regulations and bylaws.
  35|                  D. Safety, Emergency Service and Environmental Standards
  36|                 The applicant shall provide a copy of the project summary, electrical schematic, and site plan. The applicant shall
  37|                 develop an emergency response plan including showing all means of shutting down the solar installation. The
  38|                 applicant shall submit the name of the person answerable to inquires throughout the life of the installation. If the
  39|                 designated person changes, the name of the new designated person shall be submitted as an addendum.
  40|                 E. Monitoring and Maintenance
  41|                 The applicant shall submit a plan for the operation and maintenance of the installation which shall include measures for
  42|                 maintaining the site including safe access, stormwater control, structural repairs and the integrity of security measures.
  43|                 These measures must be acceptable to the Fire Chief and emergency medical services personnel. If needed, training of
  44|                 service personnel will be provided by the applicant. The ownerloperator shall be responsible for the cost of maintaining
  45|                 the installation.
  46|                 The applicant shall also submit a Monitoring/Inspection Form under the Development Plan Review during construction,
  47|                 and shall further submit a report on the condition of the structure and site by January 15th of each year.
  48|                 F. Visual Impact
  49|                                                                      84

  30|              4.15.1 Purpose
  31|                                                                   85

===PAGE 89=== (ocr, 36 lines)
   1|                   type, configuration, or size of these installations or related equipment.
   2|                   4.15.2 Definitions
   3|                   Small-Scale Ground-Mounted Photovoltaic Installation: A solar photovoltaic system that is structurally mounted on the
   4|                   ground and has a nameplate capacity under 10 kW. Large-Scale Ground-Mounted Photovoltaic Installation: A solar
   5|                   photovoltaic system that is structurally mounted on the ground and has a nameplate capacity of 10 kW or greater.
   6|                   Rated Nameplate Capacity: The maximum rated output of electric power production of the photovoltaic system in Direct
   7|                   Current (DC).
   8|                   Solar Photovoltaic Array: An arrangement of solar photovoltaic panels.
   9|                   Roof-Mounted Solar Energy Installation: Solar photovoltaic arrays placed on the roof of residences, or commercial,
  10|                  industrial or institutional buildings and Town owned municipal buildings.
  11|                   Buffer Strip: A strip of land between the solar photovoltaic arrays, and any structures accessory thereto, and the
  12|                   boundary of the parcel, reserved for plant material, berms, walls or fencing to serve as a visual barrier.
  13|                   Plant Material: Trees or shrubs of a type and height that sufficiently screen the arrays without blocking necessary sun
  14|                  that would hinder the product performance, including ground cover that would screen the lower portion of the arrays.
  15|                   4.15.3 Small-Scale Ground-Mounted Solar Photovoltaic Installations and Roof-Mounted Solar Energy Installations
  16|                   4.15.3.1 Purpose
  17|                  The purpose of section is to promote the creation of renewable energy for individual residences, commercial
  18|                  enterprises and municipal buildings, as-of-right.
  19|                  4.15.3.2 Roof-Mounted Solar Energy Installations
  20|                  Roof-Mounted Solar Energy Installations shall be located so as not to increase the total height of the structure more
  21|                  than one (1) foot above the applicable zoning regulations related to height in the District in which it is located, or such
  22|                  other height as is determined by the Building/Zoning Official to be essential for proper operation, but in no case no
  23|                  more than four (4) feet.
  24|                  4.15.3.3 Small-Scale Ground-Mounted Solar Energy Installations
  25|                  Small-Scale Ground-Mounted Photovoltaic Installations are not permitted in the Zoning Dimensional Setbacks as listed
  26|                  in Section 5.0, Subsection 5.2 of the Zoning Bylaw and may not be located closer than 25 feet from residential side-yard
  27|                  lines.
  28|                  Dimensional setbacks for Small-Scale Ground-Mounted Photovoltaic Installation shall have a front lot setback no closer
  29|                  than the existing foundation of the primary dwelling to the front property line, except when the existing foundation of
  30|                  the primary dwelling is more than 125 feet from the front property line or otherwise if determined appropriate by the
  31|                  Building/Zoning Official.
  32|                  Small-Scale Ground-Mounted Photovoltaic Installation shall be adequately screened from the neighboring lot line as
  33|                  determined by the Building/Zoning Official.
  34|                  A Small-Scale Ground-Mounted Photovoltaic Installation with twenty feet or greater in height, at its maximum extension,
  35|                  shall require a Special Permit in accordance with Section 4.15.4.3.
  36|                                                                      86

===PAGE 90=== (ocr, 36 lines)
   1|                  solar arrays, ridgeline of roof and description of the installation.
   2|                           Elevation drawings showing heights of buildings and solar arrays.
   3|                  4.15.3.5 Permitting
   4|                  Small-Scale Ground-Mounted Solar Photovoltaic Installations and Roof-Mounted Solar Energy Installations require only
   5|                  a building permit, except that a Small-Scale Ground-Mounted Solar Photovoltaic Installation twenty feet in height or
   6|                  greater, at its maximum extension, shall require a special permit in accordance with Section 4.15.4.3. All data listed

  13|                  4.15.4.1 Purpose
  14|                  The purpose of this section is to facilitate the creation of new Large-Scale Ground-Mounted Solar Photovoltaic
  15|                 Installations by providing standards for the placement, design, construction, operation, monitoring, modification and
  16|                 removal of such installations that address public safety, minimize impacts on environmental, scenic, natural and
  17|                  historic resources and to provide adequate financial assurance for the eventual decommissioning of such installations.
  18|                  Subject to the requirements below, Large-Scale Ground-Mounted Solar Photovoltaic Installations are permitted as-of-
  19|                 right in the Solar Overlay District and by special permit in any other district. The special permit granting authority for
  20|                  these purposes shall be the Planning Board.
  21|                  4.15.4.2 Solar Overlay District

  23|                  District:

  25|                          2. 671 Lancaster Avenue, 132 Acres, Map 110, Parcel 3 (Residence B)
  26|                          3. 500 Leominster Shirley Road, 17.4 Acres, Map 144, Parcel 001 (Office Park and Industrial).
  27|                 4.15.4.3 Siting By Special Permit
  28|                 Except where permitted as-of-right in the Solar Overlay District, Large-Scale Ground-Mounted Solar Photovoltaic
  29|                 Installations may be located on any lot containing more than 20 acres in any district by special permit issued by the
  30|                 Planning Board, provided however that, the requirements of Section 4.15 and the following criteria are met.
  31|                     1.   Standards: In granting the special permit, the Planning Board shall find that the permit may be granted
  32|                          without substantial detriment to the public good and without nullifying or substantially derogating from the
  33|                          intent and purpose of this bylaw, and shall find
  34|                 that:
  35|                          - the specific site is an appropriate location for such an installation;

  14|                   8.4 of the Zoning Bylaws, by the Lunenburg Planning Board. A public information meeting shall be held by said Board.

  27|                  application(s) have or will be made to such utilities for interconnection. Off-grid systems shall be exempt from this
  28|                  requirement. Reasonable efforts should be made to place all utility connections underground, depending on
  29|                  appropriate soil conditions, shape and topography of the site.
  30|                  4.15.4.6 Fees

  33|                  and inspections fees. An application for a building permit shall be accompanied by the fee required for a building
  34|                  permit. All other fees that shall be required by permitting parties (Conservation Commission, etc.) shall be administered
  35|                  according to their regulations.

  38|                  hundred (200) feet in depth in a Residential, Conservation or Recreation District and shall be 50 feet in a Commercial or
  39|                  Industrial district unless it abuts a Residential, Conservation or Recreation District in which case the buffer strip shall

   8|     photovoltaic installation shall be considered abandoned when it fails to operate for more than one year without the Absent notice of proposed date of decommissioning or written notice of extenuating circumstances, the solar
   9|     written consent of the Planning Board. The owner or operator shall physically remove the installation no more than 150 days after the date of abandonment or the proposed date of decommissioning operations. The owner or operator shall

  19|     landscape, in an amount and form determined to be reasonable by the Development Plan Review Authority but in no

  21|      facilities. The project owner/operator shall submit a fully inclusive estimate of the costs associated with removal,
  22|      prepared by a qualified engineer. The amount shall include a mechanism for calculating increased removal cost due to
  23|     inflation.

  26|      exempt from the provisions of this bylaw.

  32|      Section 4.15 SOLAR ENERGY SYSTEMS
  33|      4.15.1 Purpose
  34|                                                           91

===PAGE 95=== (ocr, 34 lines)
   1|                  The purpose of this section is to provide:
   2|                  1. As-of-right siting guidelines for all small scale ground mounted and roof mounted solar photovoltaic installations as
   3|                  accessory uses to a home, commercial business or municipal building or grounds.
   4|                  2. Provisions for a special permit process for large-scale ground mounted solar photovoltaic systems throughout the
   5|                  town as an accessory use to a home, commercial business or municipal building or grounds.
   6|                  3. As-of-right siting zones and guidelines for industrial-scale solar photovoltaic energy systems for primary use
   7|                  commercial or industrial installations where solar development may proceed without need for discretionary approval as
   8|                  set forth herein.
   9|                  The provisions set forth in this section of the Zoning Bylaw shall apply to the construction, operation, repair, andlor
  10|                  removal of solar electric installations, and to physical modifications that materially alter the type, configuration, or size
  11|                  of these installations or related equipment.
  12|                  4.15.1 Definitions
  13|                  Accessory use: any solar photovoltaic system that is an adjacent accessory use to an existing primary use of a home,
  14|                  commercial establishment or municipal building or grounds.
  15|                  Primary use: Any private or public solar photovoltaic system where the primary use of the system is to generate energy
  16|                  to be fed back into a grid for distribution.
  17|                  Small-Scale Ground-Mounted Photovoltaic Installation: A solar system that is structurally mounted on the ground and
  18|                  has a nameplate capacity under 10 kW.
  19|                  Large-Scale Ground-Mounted Photovoltaic Installation: A solar system that is structurally mounted on the ground and
  20|                  has a nameplate capacity of 10 KW to 250kW.
  21|                  Industrial-Scale Ground-Mounted Photovoltaic Installation: A solar system that is structurally mounted on the ground
  22|                  and has a nameplate capacity of more than 250kW.
  23|                  Rated Nameplate Capacity: The maximum rated output of electric power production of the photovoltaic system in Direct
  24|                  Current (DC).
  25|                  Solar Photovoltaic Array: An arrangement of solar photovoltaic panels.

  27|                  buildings and Town owned municipal buildings.
  28|                  Ground-Mounted Solar Energy Installation: Solar photovoltaic arrays placed on the grounds around residences,
  29|                  commercial buildings and Town owned buildings.

   3|     5.0., Subsection 5.2. of the Zoning Bylaw and may not be located closer than 25 feet from residential yard lines. Small-scale ground mounted solar equipment is not permitted in the Zoning Dimensional Setbacks as listed in Section
   4|     Small-scale ground-mounted equipment shall be adequately screened from the neighboring lot line as determined by the Building/Zoning Official. Screening height should reflect the site as determined by Building/Zoning Official.

   8|     (a) Sun and shadow diagrams specific to the proposed installation to determine the solar access.
   9|     (b) Detailed information, including maps, plans or dimensional sketches showing proposed location of the solar installation, including any setbacks from property lines or distances from structures which are used for habitation on
  10|      adjacent properties.
  11|      (c) Site drawings showing the building footprint, property lines, location and the dimensions of solar collectors, ridgeline of roof and description of the solar system. Siting of small Ground Mounted Arrays dimensional setbacks

  15|       4.15.3.5 Permitting
  16|       Small-scale solar equipment requires only a building permit. All data listed above shall be submitted to the Building/Zoning Official. All other necessary permits, such as electrical shall be obtained through individual inspectors.
  17|      4.15.3.6 As Built Plans As-built plans shall be submitted prior to final inspection to the Buildng Inspector and copied to the Planning Board.
  18|       4.15.4 Large Scale and Industrial Scale Solar Energy Systems
  19|       4.15.4.1 Purpose
  20|       The purpose of this section is to facilitate the creation of new Large-Scale and Industrial-Scale Ground-Mounted Solar Photovoltaic Installations by providing standards for the placement, design, construction, operation, monitoring,
  21|       modification and removal of such installations that address public safety, minimize impacts on environmental, scenic, natural and historic resources and to provide
  22|       adequate financial assurance for the eventual decommissioning of such installations.
  23|       The provisions set forth in this Section shall apply to the construction, operation and/or repair of all large-scale and Industrial Scale ground-mounted solar photovoltaic installations.
  24|       4.15.4.2 As-of-right Overlay District
  25|                                                           93

===PAGE 97=== (ocr, 42 lines)
   1|                 4.15.4.3 Development Plan Review
   2|                 All large-scale and industrial-scale ground-mounted solar photovoltaic installations shall require Development Plan
   3|                 Review, under Section 8.4 of the Zoning Bylaws, by the Lunenburg Planning Board. A public information meeting shall
   4|                 be held by said Board. The Building/Zoning Official shall not issue a building permit unless and until the Planning
   5|                 Board submits an approved Development Plan Review Document to the Building/Zoning Official.
   6|                 A building permit will be issued by the Building/Zoning Official that shows evidence that the project is consistent with
   7|                 state and federal building codes, the findings and directives of the Development Plan Review, and local bylaws and
   8|                 regulations, including those set forth by the Conservation Commission. As-built plans shall be submitted prior to final
   9|                 inspection to the Building /Zoning Officer and copies to
  10|                 the Planning Board
  11|                 4.15.4.4 Special Permit Application
  12|                 All large-scale ground mounted solar photovoltaic installations not contained within the As-of-right overlay district
  13|                 described in section 4.15.4.2 of this by-law are subject to special permit. The special permit granting authority shall be
  14|                 the Planning Board. All special permit applications shall be subject to Development Plan Review as described in
  15|                 section 4.15.4.3 above. In addition, the special permit
  16|                 granting authority may impose other restrictions or required documentation or limit required documentation at their
  17|                 discretion on a plan by-plan basis.
  18|                 4.15.4.4 Utility Notification
  19|                 Evidence shall be provided at time of the application for the Development Plan Review that the utility company that
  20|                 operates the electrical grid where the installation is be located has been informed of the intent of a solar photovoltaic
  21|                 installation and that approval to connect to the grid has been granted or appropriate application(s) have or will be been
  22|                 made to such utilities for interconnection. Off-grid
  23|                 systems shall be exempt from this requirement. Reasonable efforts should be made to place all utility connections
  24|                 underground, depending on appropriate soil conditions, shape and topography of the site.
  25|                 4.15.4.5 Fees
  26|                 An application for a Development Plan Review shall be accompanied by the required fee and a tri-party account (in-
  27|                 house escrow account w/Plannin Board, Developer and Town Treasurer) for engineering review, monitoring, and
  28|                 inspections fees. An application for a building permit shall be accompanied by the fee required for a building permit. All
  29|                 other fees that shall be required by permitting parties (Conservation Commission, etc.) shall be administered according
  30|                 to their regulations.
  31|                 4.15.4.6 Setbacks and Buffer Strips
  32|                 Setbacks in all Districts: All setbacks are measured from the lot line
  33|                 Front Yard: The Front Yard shall be at least 50 feet and shall be an adequate buffer strip, provided, however, that when
  34|                 the lot is in a residential district, abuts a residence, a conservation district or a recreation district the front lot depth
  35|                 shall not be less than 250 feet (100 feet of which shall be a buffer strip)
  36|                 Side Yard: Each Side Yard shall be at least 50 feet and shall be an adequate buffer strip, provided, however, that when
  37|                the lot is in a residential district, abuts a residence, a conservation district or a recreation district the side lot depth
  38|                 shall not be less than 250 feet (100 feet of which shall be buffer strip).
  39|                 Rear Yard: The Rear Yard shall be at least 50 feet and shall be an adequate buffer strip, provided, however, that when
  40|                the lot is in a residential district, abuts a residence, a conservation district or a recreation district the rear lot depth
  41|                shall not be less than 250 feet(100 feet of which shall be buffer strip).
  42|                                                                    94

   1|        plan shall be submitted under the Development Plan Review.
   2|       In addition to documents required under the Zoning Bylaw, Section 8.4 entitled Development Plan Review, the following 4.15.4.8 Required Documents
   3|       will be required for Large-Scale and Industrial-Scale Ground-Mounted Solar Photovoltaic Installation:
   4|        All plans and maps shall be prepared, stamped and signed by a professional engineer licensed to practice in Massachusetts.

   6|        Proposed changes to landscape of site, including grading, vegetation, clearing, planting, screening vegetation or structures.
   7|        Drawings of the solar photovoltaic installation showing the proposed layout of the system and any potential shading from nearby structures.
   8|        One or three phase line electrical diagrams detailing the solar photovoltaic installation, associated components and electrical interconnection methods with all National Electrical Code compliant disconnects and overcurrent devices.
   9|        Documentation of the major system components to be used, including the electric generating PV panels, mounting system, inverter, etc.
  10|        name of contact person, address,& phone # for information on the constructed project and name of proposed system Name, of property owner, address & telephone number, name of lessor or lessee & address & telephone number, and
  11|         installer, address & phone #..
  12|         Zoning District designation for parcel of land comprising the project site.
  13|         4.15.4.9 Site Control The applicant shall submit documentation of actual or prospective access and control of the project site sufficient to
  14|         allow for construction and operation of the proposed solar photovoltaic installation. 4.15.4.10 Design Standards
  15|         Lighting of solar photovoltaic installations shall be consistent with local, state and federal law. Lighting of other parts a. Lighting
  16|         of the installation, such as any appurtenant structures, shall be limited to that required for safety and operational purposes and shall be reasonably shielded from abutting properties. Where feasible, any required lighting shall be
  17|          directed downward and shall incorporate full cut off fixtures to reduce light pollution.
  18|          b. Signage
  19|          Signage size shall comply with the Zoning Bylaw and shall not be used for displaying any advertising except to identify the owner and/or operator of the solar installation and a 24-hour emergency contact telephone number.
  20|         •c. Security Cameras
  21|          the activity of any abutting residential lot. To protect the privacy of all residents, security or surveillance cameras shall be shielded or prevented from recording
  22|                                                              95

===PAGE 99=== (ocr, 41 lines)
   1|                 The applicant shall provide a copy of the project summary, electrical schematic, and site plan. The applicant shall
   2|                 develop an emergency response plan including showing all means of shutting down the solar installation. The
   3|                 applicant shall submit the name of the person answerable to inquires throughout the life of the installation. If the
   4|                 designated person changes, the name of the new designated person shall be submitted as an addendum.
   5|                 f. Monitoring and Maintenance
   6|                 The applicant shall submit a plan for the operation and maintenance of the installation which shall include measures for
   7|                 maintaining the site including safe access, stormwater control, structural repairs and the integrity of security measures.
   8|                 These measures must be acceptable to the Fire Chief and emergency medical services personnel. If needed, training
   9|                 will be provided by the applicant. The
  10|                 owner/operator shall be responsible for the cost of maintaining the installation.
  11|                 The applicant shall also submit a Monitoring/Inspection Form under the Development Plan Review during construction,
  12|                 and shall further submit a report on the condition of the structure and site by January 15th of each year.
  13|                 4.15.4.11 Modifications
  14|                 All modification requests to a solar photovoltaic installation, including changes in arrays, addition to number of arrays
  15|                 or change in placement made after issuance of the required building permit shall require review through the
  16|                 Development Plan Review for compliance with this Bylaw.
  17|                 4.15.4.12 Abandonment and Removal
  18|                 The owner or operator shall physically remove the installation no more than 150 days after the date of
  19|                 decommissioning operations. The owner or operator shall notify the Planning Board by certified mail of their proposed
  20|                 date of discontinued operations and plans for removal. The notification shall consist of the methodology of physical
  21|                 removal of all structures, equipment, security barriers and transmission lines, disposal of all solid and hazardous waste
  22|                 and stabilization or re-vegetation of the site. Landscaping, etc. may be left upon approval of the Planning Board. If the
  23|                 owner or operator fails to remove the installation in accordance with the above criteria, the Town may, after the receipt
  24|                 of an appropriate court order or consent of the property owner enter the property and physically remove
  25|                 the installation at the owner's expense. As a condition of Development Plan Review, a property owner shall agree to
  26|                 allow the Town entry to remove an abandoned or decommissioned installation. The cost for the removal will be charged
  27|                 to the property owner in accordance with the provisions of M.G.L. 139, Section 3A as a tax lien on the property.
  28|                 4.15.4.13 Financial Surety
  29|                 Proponents of large-scale and industrial-scale solar photovoltaic projects shall provide a form of surety, either through
  30|                 an escrow account, bond or otherwise to cover the cost of removal in the event the Town must remove the installation
  31|                 and remediate the landscape, in an amount and form determined to be reasonable by the Development Plan Review
  32|                 Authority but in no event to exceed more than 125 percent of the cost of removal and compliance with the additional
  33|                 requirement set forth herein, as determined by the project proponent. Such surety will not be required for municipally
  34|                 or state owned facilities. The project owner/operator shall submit a fully inclusive estimate of the costs associated with
  35|                 removal, prepared by a qualified engineer. The amount shall include a mechanism for calculating increased removal
  36|                 cost due to inflation.
